v\in C(\mathbb{T})\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}\psi\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}n\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 2.3

(
\smash{\frac{1}{3}}
-Hölder regularity)
.

Assume the potential
v:\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{R}
is Lipschitz continuous, i.e., there exists
\mathrm{c}_{v}=\mathrm{c}>0
such that for all
\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\in\mathbb{T}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\lvert v(\omega_{1})-v(\omega_{2})\rvert\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}\lvert\omega_{1}-\omega_{2}\rvert\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Then

(a)

\tau
satisfies a power Lipschitz continuity condition:
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle d(-n\cdot_{\tau}x,-n\cdot_{\tau}y)\leq(\smash{\frac{n(n-1)}{2}}+\lvert n\rvert+1)d(x,y)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(b)

k
satisfies a Lipschitz continuity condition:
for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\lvert k(m,x)-k(m,y)\rvert\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}\delta_{0}(m)d(x,y)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(c)

k
satisfies a linear decay condition:
for each
r\geq 0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\sup_{x\in Z}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\lVert k(m,-(\cdot)\cdot_{\tau}x)\min\{\lvert m\rvert,r\}\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(d)

\langle(\mathbb{Z},\ell),\#\rangle
is strictly-polynomial-growing:
for each
r\geq 1\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

r\leq\#B(r)\leq 3r\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(e)

For all
X,Y\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\smash{{r\geq 1\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\dist_{\mathsf{H}}(\spec(A_{X}),\spec(A_{Y}))\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}(\smash{\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}+r+1)d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)+\frac{2}{r}(\frac{3^{4}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}}

and there exists
\delta(\mathrm{c})>0
(independent of
X
and
Y
) such that

\textstyle d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)\leq{\min\{\delta(\mathrm{c}),1\}}\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\dist_{\mathsf{H}}(\spec(A_{X}),\spec(A_{Y}))\leq Cd_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)^{\frac{1}{3}}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

where
C\coloneqq\min\{\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}(\tfrac{\delta^{\frac{2}{3}}}{2}+\tfrac{\delta^{\frac{1}{3}}}{2}+1)+\tfrac{2}{\delta^{\frac{1}{3}}}(\tfrac{3^{4}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}:\delta>0\}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
\delta(\mathrm{c})
minimizes
C\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem
2.3
(
a
,
b
,
c
,
d
) are easy to see and come in handy since Theorem
2.3
(
e
) follows from a combination of (
a
–
d
) and Lemma
4.3
(where amenability twinkles) and Lemma
4.5
.
We remark that this operator family can be found within
[
19
,
20
,
50
]
and this regularity result is new.
We forgo generalizing Theorem
1.3
(where the group action is instead assumed to be power Lipschitz).

2.4.
One-dimensional Discrete Limit-periodic Schrödinger Operators

We demonstrate that our unified theory applies for this titular setting provided a lenient condition replaces condition
(
i
)
within Theorem
1.3
; see Theorem
2.4
(
a
).

Let
\langle\mathbb{Z},\#\rangle
be the one-dimensional lattice endowed with the counting measure.
Let
\ell
be the metric on
\mathbb{Z}
defined by

\textstyle\ell(n,m)=\lvert-n+m\rvert

for every
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define the space
\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}\coloneqq[0,1]^{\mathbb{Z}}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
d
be the metric on
\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}
defined by

\textstyle d(x,y)=\sum_{m\geq 0}\smash{\frac{1}{2^{m}}}\max\{\lvert x_{m}-y_{m}\rvert,\lvert x_{-m}-y_{-m}\rvert\}

for every
x,y\in\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
\langle\smash[t]{\bar{Z}},(\mathbb{Z},\tau)\rangle
be the dynamical system defined by

\textstyle-n\cdot_{\tau}x=(m\mapsto x(n+m))\eqqcolon T^{-n}x

for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}x\in\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define the phase (sub)space
Z\coloneqq\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}\cap\{\textup{limit-periodic}\}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
i.e., for each
x\in\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

x\in Z
if and only if there exists a sequence
\{y_{j}\}\subseteq\smash[t]{\bar{Z}}
such that
\#\{-n\cdot_{\tau}y_{j}:n\in\mathbb{Z}\}<+\infty
for every
j
and
\lVert x-y_{j}\rVert_{\infty}\to 0
as
j\to+\infty\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
v\in C(Z)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
the kernel
k_{v}=k:\mathbb{Z}\times Z\to\mathbb{C}
by

\textstyle k(m,x)=\begin{cases}1&\textup{if}~m\in\{-1,1\}\\
v(x)&\textup{if}~m=0\\
0&\textup{else}\end{cases}

for every
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}x\in Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define the operator family
\{A_{v,x}\}_{v\in C(Z),x\in Z}\subseteq\mathscr{L}(\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z}))
by

\textstyle(A_{x}\psi)(n)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}k(-n+m,-n\cdot_{\tau}x)\psi(m)=\psi(n+1)+\psi(n-1)+v(T^{-n}x)\psi(n)

for every
v\in C(Z)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}\psi\in\ell^{2}(\mathbb{Z})\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}n\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 2.4

(log-Hölder regularity)
.

Assume the potential
v:Z\to\mathbb{R}
is Lipschitz continuous, i.e., there exists
\mathrm{c}_{v}=\mathrm{c}>0
such that for all
x,y\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\lvert v(x)-v(y)\rvert\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}d(x,y)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Then

(a)

\tau
satisfies an exponential Lipschitz continuity condition:
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle d(-n\cdot_{\tau}x,-n\cdot_{\tau}y)\leq 2^{\lvert n\rvert}d(x,y)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(b)

k
satisfies a Lipschitz continuity condition:
for each
m\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\lvert k(m,x)-k(m,y)\rvert\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}\delta_{0}(m)d(x,y)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(c)

k
satisfies a linear decay condition:
for each
r\geq 0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\sup_{x\in Z}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\lVert k(m,-(\cdot)\cdot_{\tau}x)\min\{\lvert m\rvert,r\}\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(d)

\langle(\mathbb{Z},\ell),\#\rangle
is strictly-polynomial-growing:
for each
r\geq 1\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

r\leq\#B(r)\leq 3r\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(e)

For all
X,Y\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\smash{{r\geq 1\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\dist_{\mathsf{H}}(\spec(A_{X}),\spec(A_{Y}))\leq\mathrm{c}\mspace{1muminus1mu}2^{r}d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)+\frac{2}{r}(\frac{3^{4}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}}

and for each
\rho\in(0,1)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists
\delta(\rho)>0
(independent of
X
and
Y
) such that

\textstyle\hskip 0.0ptd_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)\leq{\min\{\delta(\rho),1\}}\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\dist_{\mathsf{H}}(\spec(A_{X}),\spec(A_{Y}))\leq C(\log_{2}(({\scriptstyle d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)^{\rho}})^{-1}))^{-1}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

where
C\coloneqq\mathrm{c}+\tfrac{2}{1}(\tfrac{3^{4}}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
\delta(\rho)=\delta
satisfies an inequality:
\delta^{1-\rho}\leq(\log_{2}(({\scriptstyle\delta^{\rho}})^{-1}))^{-1}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem
2.4
(
a
,
b
,
c
,
d
) are easy to see and come in handy since Theorem
2.4
(
e
) follows from a combination of (
a
–
d
) and Lemma
4.3
(where amenability twinkles) and Lemma
4.5
.
We remark that this operator family can be found within
[
2
,
8
,
35
]
and this regularity result is new.
We forgo generalizing Theorem
1.3
(where the group action is instead assumed to be exponential Lipschitz).

3.
Preliminaries

3.1.
Locally-compact Hausdorff Groups

We provide a terse exposition of
topological groups.
The focus is on
the existence of a translation-invariant sigma-finite measure and
the existence of a translation-invariant proper metric.
The topics on unimodularity and amenability are principal axes.

Say
G
is a
topological group
if

G
is a group with a topology
\mathcal{T}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where
the group product and the group inverse are continuous.
Let
G
be a topological group.
Assume
G
is both Hausdorff and locally-compact.
Say
\lambda
is a
Radon measure
on
G
if

\lambda
is a nonzero Borel measure on
G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where

\lambda
is outer regular for Borel sets and inner regular for open sets and finite for compact sets.
Say
\lambda
is a
left Haar measure
on
G
if

\lambda
is a left-invariant Radon measure on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
G
be a locally-compact Hausdorff group.

(a)

There exists a left Haar measure
\lambda
on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
\lambda
is unique modulo scaling.

(b)

If
G
is sigma-compact, then
\lambda
is sigma-finite and
\lambda
is inner regular for Borel sets.

(c)

If
G
is first-countable, then there exists a left-invariant
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(d)

If
G
is second-countable, then there exists a left-invariant proper
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(e)

G
is sigma-compact and
G
is first-countable if and only if
G
is second-countable.

The proof of Theorem
3.1
(
a
,
b
,
c
,
d
)
can be found within
[
32
,
65
,
55
,
60
]
.
Theorem
3.1
(
e
) follows from
the observation that every sigma-compact space is a Lindelöf space and
the observation that, for metric spaces, Lindelöfness and separability and second-countability are equivalent;
see
[
64
]
.

3.1.1.
Unimodularity

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be the collection of all Borel subsets of
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
g\in G
and
B\in\mathcal{B}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\lambda^{g}(B)\coloneqq\lambda(Bg)
and
\lambda^{*}(B)\coloneqq\lambda(B^{\smash{-1}})\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe
\lambda^{g}
is a left Haar measure on
G
and there exists
c(g)>0
such that
\lambda^{g}=c(g)\lambda\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

It can be shown that
c:G\to(0,+\infty)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
called the modular function, is a continuous homomorphism.
Say
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is
unimodular
if

c
is identically
1
or, equivalently,
\lambda^{g}=\lambda
for every
g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

It can be shown that if
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is unimodular, then
\lambda^{*}=\lambda\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe if
G
is discrete or
G
is abelian or
G
is compact, then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is unimodular.
To see how unimodularity follows from
G/[G,G]
being compact,
the image of
[G,G]\coloneqq\overline{\{[g,h]:g,h\in G\}}
under
c
is trivial since
c
is a homomorphism and

c(G)=c(G/[G,G])
is compact since
c
is continuous and
the only compact subgroup of
(0,+\infty)
is
\{1\}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

The details of this subsubsection and more examples can be found within
[
32
]
such as connected Lie groups that are nilpotent or semisimple.

3.1.2.
Amenability

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Say
\mathfrak{m}
is a
mean
on
G
if

\mathfrak{m}
is a nonzero bounded linear functional on
L^{\infty}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where

\mathfrak{m}(\varphi)\geq 0
for every
\varphi\in L_{+}^{\infty}(G)
and
\mathfrak{m}(\mathbbm{1}_{G})=1\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Say
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is
amenable
if
a left-invariant mean on
G
exists or, equivalently, for each
\varepsilon>0
and for each nonempty compact subset
F
of
G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists a compactly-supported continuous nonzero
\chi\in L_{+}^{2}(G)
such that
\sup_{h\in F}\lVert\cramped{\chi^{h}}-\chi\rVert<\varepsilon\lVert\chi\rVert\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Here
{\cramped{\chi^{h}}:G\to[0,+\infty):g\mapsto\chi(gh)}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

It can be shown that if
G
is abelian or
G
is compact, then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is amenable.
Also, if
G
is solvable, then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is amenable since
G
is an extension of an amenable group by an amenable closed normal subgroup.
We remark that if
G
has a closed subgroup isomorphic to the free group with two generators
\mathrm{F}_{2}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is not amenable.
There is an extent to which nonamenability is related to the presence of
\mathrm{F}_{2}
; see
chapter 3
within
[
58
]
:
if
G
is almost-connected, then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is amenable if and only if
G
does not have a closed subgroup isomorphic to
\mathrm{F}_{2}
;
if
G
is a connected Lie group, then
G
is solvable (thus amenable) if and only if
G
does not have a closed subgroup isomorphic to
\mathrm{F}_{2}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

The details of this subsubsection and more examples can be found within
[
57
,
58
]
such as groups that are uniformly-distributed or polynomial-growing.

3.2.
Dynamically-defined Operator Families

We provide a terse exposition of
operator families induced by elements of a
C^{*}
-algebra itself induced by a topological dynamical system.
The focus is on their covariant representation and properties, e.g., uniform boundedness and strong continuity.

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Define, for
\varphi\in L^{1}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\int\varphi(h)dh\coloneqq\int\varphi d\lambda\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle
be a compact Hausdorff space endowed with a left continuous action.
Specifically,
\alpha
is a left continuous action if and only if
\alpha:G\times Z\to Z
is continuous and
\alpha(e,x)=x
for every
x\in Z
and
\alpha(g,\alpha(h,x))=\alpha(gh,x)
for every
g,h\in G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}x\in Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
g\in G
and
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\alpha_{g}x\coloneqq\alpha(g,x)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
g\in G
and
f\in C(Z)
and
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

(\alpha_{g}f)(x)\coloneqq f(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Here
C(Z)

(\text{or }C_{b}(Z))
is the unital
C^{*}
-algebra of all (bounded) continuous complex-valued functions on
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

[]_{x}:C_{c}(G,C(Z))\to\mathscr{L}(L^{2}(G))
by

\textstyle[f_{(\cdot)}]_{x}=\int d\lambda(h)[(\alpha_{(\cdot)}f_{h})(x)]R_{h}=(f_{g^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x))

for every
f_{(\cdot)}\in C_{c}(G,C(Z))\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Here
C_{c}(G,C(Z))
is the linear space of all compactly-supported continuous
C(Z)
-valued functions on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
[(\alpha_{(\cdot)}f_{h})(x)]:L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
a multiplication operator, is defined by
([(\alpha_{(\cdot)}f_{h})(x)]\psi)(g)=f_{h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\psi(g)
for every
\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
R_{h}:L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
called the right regular representation, is defined by
(R_{h}\psi)(g)=\psi(gh)
for every
\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
(f_{g^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x))
is a matrix whose
(g,h)
-th component is
f_{g^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

More conventionally,

\textstyle([f_{(\cdot)}]_{x}\psi)(g)=\int f_{h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\psi(gh)dh=\int f_{g^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\psi(h)dh

for every
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}f_{(\cdot)}\in C_{c}(G,C(Z))\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe
[]_{x}
is linear and
[f_{(\cdot)}]_{x}
is linear.
Also,
U_{j^{\smash{-1}}}[(\alpha_{(\cdot)}f_{h})(x)]U_{j}=[(\alpha_{(\cdot)}f_{h})(\alpha_{j^{\smash{-1}}}x)]
and
[U_{j},R_{h}]=0\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Here
U_{j}:L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
called the left regular representation, is defined by

(U_{j}\psi)(g)=\psi(j^{\smash{-1}}g)

for every
\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

We remark that
C_{c}(G,C(Z))
is a
∗
-algebra and
\lVert f_{(\cdot)}*\smash[t]{\tilde{f}}_{(\cdot)}\rVert\leq\lVert f_{(\cdot)}\rVert\lVert\smash[t]{\tilde{f}}_{(\cdot)}\rVert
and
\lVert f_{(\cdot)}\!{}^{*}*f_{(\cdot)}\rVert=\lVert f_{(\cdot)}\rVert^{2}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where

(f_{(\cdot)}*\smash[t]{\tilde{f}}_{(\cdot)})_{h}(x)\coloneqq\int f_{j}(x)\smash[t]{\tilde{f}}_{j^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{j^{\smash{-1}}}x)dj\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}(f_{(\cdot)}\!{}^{*})_{h}(x)\coloneqq\overline{f_{h^{\smash{-1}}}(\alpha_{h^{\smash{-1}}}x)}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}\lVert f_{(\cdot)}\rVert\coloneqq\sup_{x\in Z}\lVert[f_{(\cdot)}]_{x}\rVert\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
C_{c}(G,C(Z))
embeds into a
C^{*}
-algebra, denoted
C(Z)\rtimes_{\alpha}G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where

\textstyle\spec(f_{(\cdot)})=\overline{\bigcup_{x\in Z}\spec([f_{(\cdot)}]_{x})}

for normal elements; see
[
11
,
17
,
59
,
22
]
.
The
C^{*}
-algebra
C(Z)\rtimes_{\alpha}G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
called a reduced crossed product, and its
K
-theory were utilized to prove the Gap Labeling Theorems; see
[
11
,
12
,
16
,
17
,
52
]
:
if
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is both unimodular and amenable and
Z
is second-countable, then for each ergodic left-invariant Borel probability measure
\mu
on
Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists a countable subgroup of
\mathbb{R}
containing all gap labels.
The 1985 result by J. Bellissard; R. Lima; D. Testard
[
11
]
and the 1986 result by J. Bellissard
[
12
]
considered the case where
G=\mathbb{Z}^{d}
or
G=\mathbb{R}^{d}
or
G
is both discrete and amenable and utilized that
G
sometimes has a regular Følner sequence so that the pointwise ergodic theorem holds for the ‘time-average’ approximations of the ‘space-average’ integrated density of states—whose image over the gaps in the spectra of self-adjoint elements of
C(Z)\rtimes_{\alpha}G
are precisely the gap labels.
The existence of regular Følner sequences is not known for amenable groups with exponential growth
[
56
]
, but the 2001 result by E. Lindenstrauss
[
52
]
establishes that amenable groups always have tempered Følner sequences.
We remark that tempered Følner sequences are suitable.

This subsection is the only place where
C^{*}
-algebras are mentioned, and it is done so to expose the spectrum map

\textstyle X\mapsto\overline{\bigcup_{x\in X}\spec([f_{(\cdot)}]_{x})}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

where
X
is a subsystem of
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Specifically,
X
is a subsystem of
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle
if and only if
X
is a left-invariant nonempty closed subset of
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Let
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle
be a topological space endowed with a left continuous action.
Let
f_{(\cdot)}\in C_{c}(G,C_{b}(Z))\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

{A}_{x}:L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)
by

\textstyle({A}_{x}\psi)(g)=\int f_{h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\psi(gh)dh=\int f_{g^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g^{\smash{-1}}}x)\psi(h)dh

for every
\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
(h,x)\in G\times Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g_{\mathtt{1}},g_{\mathtt{2}}\in G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

k(g_{\mathtt{1}}^{\smash{-1}}h,g_{\mathtt{2}}^{\smash{-1}}x)\coloneqq f_{g_{\mathtt{1}}^{\smash{-1}}h}(\alpha_{g_{\mathtt{2}}^{\smash{-1}}}x)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
\mathscr{S}
be the collection of all subsystems of
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
X\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\spec(A_{X})\coloneqq\overline{\bigcup_{x\in X}\spec(A_{x})}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(a)

f_{h}(x)=k(h,x)=k:G\times Z\to\mathbb{C}
is measurable and
\sup_{x\in Z}\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\rVert_{\infty}dh<+\infty\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(b)

If
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is unimodular, then for all
x,y\in Z
and for each
\psi\in L^{2}(G)
and for each
j\in G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\lVert{A}_{x}\psi\rVert\leq

\displaystyle(\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\mathbbm{1}_{\supp(\psi)h^{\smash{-1}}}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}dh)\lVert\psi\rVert\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\lVert{A}_{x}\!{}^{*}\psi\rVert\leq

\displaystyle(\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\mathbbm{1}_{\supp(\psi)}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}dh)\lVert\psi\rVert\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\lVert({A}_{y}-{A}_{x})\psi\rVert\leq

\displaystyle(\int\lVert(k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}y)-k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x))\mathbbm{1}_{\supp(\psi)h^{\smash{-1}}}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}dh)\lVert\psi\rVert\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\displaystyle\textstyle\lVert({A}_{y}-{A}_{x})^{*}\psi\rVert\leq

\displaystyle(\int\lVert(k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}y)-k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x))\mathbbm{1}_{\supp(\psi)}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}dh)\lVert\psi\rVert\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\displaystyle\textstyle U_{j^{\smash{-1}}}{A}_{x}U_{j}=

\displaystyle{A}_{j^{\smash{-1}}x}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(c)

If
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is unimodular and
A_{x}
is normal for every
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
then for each
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle\lim_{y\xrightarrow{\smash{Z}}x}\sup_{E\in\spec({A}_{x})}\dist(E,\spec({A}_{y}))=0

and
\spec(A_{x})=\spec(A_{X(x)})\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
where
X(x)\coloneqq\overline{*}{\{g^{\smash{-1}}x:g\in G\}}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Lemma
3.2
(
a
) follows from

f_{(\cdot)}\in C_{c}(G,C_{b}(Z))
and
f_{h}(x)=k(h,x)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

The proof of—a suitable generalization of—Lemma
3.2
(
b
,
c
) can be found within
section
5
.

3.3.
Hausdorff Distance

Let
Z
be a metrizable space.
Let
d
be a
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Specifically, for each metric
d
on
Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

d
is
\mathcal{T}
-generating if and only if the balls
\{y\in Z:d(y,x)<r\}
are open and generate the topology on
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also, the balls are precompact if and only if
d
is proper.
Let
S_{\mathtt{1}}
and
S_{\mathtt{2}}
be subsets of
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

The
Hausdorff distance
between
S_{\mathtt{1}}
and
S_{\mathtt{2}}
with respect to
d
is

\textstyle\max\{\sup_{s\in S_{\mathtt{1}}}d(s,S_{\mathtt{2}}),\sup_{s\in S_{\mathtt{2}}}d(s,S_{\mathtt{1}})\}\eqqcolon d_{\mathsf{H}}(S_{\mathtt{1}},S_{\mathtt{2}})\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
G
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group.
Let
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle
be a compact metrizable (or second-countable compact Hausdorff) space endowed with a left continuous action.
Let
\mathscr{S}
be the collection of all subsystems of
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
d
be a
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Then
(\mathscr{S},d_{\mathsf{H}})
is a compact metric space.

The proof of Theorem
3.3
can be found within
[
22
]
.

3.4.
Strict Polynomial Growth

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Let
\ell
be a left-invariant proper
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
g\in G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\lvert g\rvert\coloneqq\ell(g,e)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
r\geq 0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

B(r)\coloneqq\{g\in G:\lvert g\rvert<r\}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Say
\langle(G,\ell),\lambda\rangle
is
strictly-polynomial-growing
if there exist
b\geq 1
and
c_{1}\geq c_{0}>0
such that for each
r\geq 1\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\textstyle c_{0}r^{b}\leq\lambda(B(r))\leq c_{1}r^{b}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with a left Haar measure.
Let
\ell
be a left-invariant proper
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Assume
\langle(G,\ell),\lambda\rangle
is strictly-polynomial-growing.
Then
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is both unimodular and amenable.

The proof of Theorem
3.4
can be found within
[
56
]
.

4.
Proof of Main Results

Let
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
be
a second-countable locally-compact Hausdorff group endowed with
a left Haar measure.
Let
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle
be
a metrizable space endowed with
a left continuous action.
Let
k:G\times Z\to\mathbb{C}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

where
k
is measurable and

\sup_{x\in Z}\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\rVert_{\infty}dh<+\infty\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

{A}_{x}:L^{2}(G)\to L^{2}(G)
by

\textstyle(A_{x}\psi)(g)=\int k(h,g^{\smash{-1}}x)\psi(gh)dh=\int k(g^{\smash{-1}}h,g^{\smash{-1}}x)\psi(h)dh

for every
\psi\in L^{2}(G)\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss{,}}\hskip 1.66669pt\mspace{3muplus0muminus3mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{2muplus0muminus2mu}}{\textup{,}\mspace{1muplus0muminus1mu}}g\in G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
\mathscr{S}
be the collection of all subsystems of
\langle Z,(G,\alpha)\rangle\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Define, for
X\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}

\spec(A_{X})\coloneqq\overline{\bigcup_{x\in X}\spec(A_{x})}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
\ell
be a left-invariant proper
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
G\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Let
d
be a
\mathcal{T}
-generating metric on
Z\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Theorem 4.1
.

Assume
\langle G,\lambda\rangle
is unimodular,
A_{x}
is normal for every
x\in Z\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
and the following.

(i)

\alpha
satisfies a uniform continuity condition:
for each
\varepsilon>0
and for each nonempty compact subset
K
of
G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists
\delta>0
such that

d(y,x)<\delta\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\sup_{g\in K}d(g^{\smash{-1}}y,g^{\smash{-1}}x)<\varepsilon\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(ii)

k
satisfies a uniform continuity condition:
for each
\varepsilon>0
and for each nonempty compact subset
F
of
G\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists
\delta>0
such that

d(y,x)<\delta\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\int\lvert k(h,y)-k(h,x)\rvert\mathbbm{1}_{F}(h)dh<\varepsilon\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

(iii)

k
satisfies a uniform decay condition:
for each
\varepsilon>0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists a nonempty compact subset
F
of
G
such that

\sup_{x\in Z}\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\mathbbm{1}_{G\setminus F}(h)\rVert_{\infty}dh<\varepsilon\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Fix
X\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Then for each
\varepsilon>0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists
\delta>0
such that for each
Y\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
if
d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)<\delta\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
then
\sup_{E\in\spec(A_{X})}\dist(E,\spec(A_{Y}))<\varepsilon\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
i.e.,

\textstyle\lim_{Y\xrightarrow{\smash{\mathscr{S}}}X}\sup_{E\in\spec(A_{X})}\dist(E,\spec(A_{Y}))=0\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Proof.
It suffices to show that
for each
E\in\spec(A_{X})
and for each
\varepsilon>0\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
there exists
\delta>0
such that for each
Y\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
if
d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)<\delta\mathchoice{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{,\kern-3.79996pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule[-1.94247pt]{1.47774pt}{1.1611pt}}\hss}\kern 2.13324pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss,}}{\text{,}}{\text{,}}
then
\dist(E,\spec(A_{Y}))<\varepsilon
since
\spec(A_{X})
is compact.
Fix
E\in\spec(A_{X})
and
\varepsilon>0\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe there exists a nonempty compact subset
F
of
G
such that

\textstyle\sup_{x\in Z}\int\lVert k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x)\mathbbm{1}_{G\setminus F}(h)\rVert_{\infty}dh<\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon

since
k
satisfies a uniform decay condition.
Observe there exist
x\in X
and
E^{\prime}\in\spec(A_{x})
such that
\lvert E-E^{\prime}\rvert<\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Without loss of generality, assume
E^{\prime}\notin\spec(A_{Y})\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe
A_{x}
is normal and, by Weyl’s criterion, there exists a nonzero vector
\varphi
such that
\lVert(A_{x}-E^{\prime})\varphi\rVert<\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon\lVert\varphi\rVert\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Without loss of generality, assume
\varphi
is both continuous and compactly-supported.
Define
K\coloneqq\supp(\varphi)\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe there exists
\delta>0
such that

\textstyle d(y,x)<2\delta\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\Rightarrow\mspace{4muplus0muminus4mu}\int\lVert(k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}y)-k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x))\mathbbm{1}_{K}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{F}(h)dh<\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon

since the group action and
k
satisfy a uniform continuity condition.
Fix
Y\in\mathscr{S}\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Assume
d_{\mathsf{H}}(X,Y)<\delta\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Observe there exists
y\in Y
such that
d(x,y)<d(x,Y)+\delta<2\delta\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}

Also,
A_{y}
is normal and

\textstyle\lVert(A_{y}-E^{\prime})^{-1}\rVert^{-1}\lVert\varphi\rVert\leq\lVert(A_{y}-E^{\prime})\varphi\rVert=\lVert(A_{y}-E^{\prime})^{*}\varphi\rVert<(\lVert(A_{y}-A_{x})^{*}\smash{\frac{\varphi}{\lVert\varphi\rVert}}\rVert+\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon)\lVert\varphi\rVert

and

\displaystyle\dist(E,\spec(A_{Y}))

\displaystyle<\dist(E^{\prime},\spec(A_{y}))+\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\lVert(A_{y}-E^{\prime})^{-1}\rVert^{-1}+\smash{\frac{1}{5}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\lVert(A_{y}-A_{x})^{*}\smash{\frac{\varphi}{\lVert\varphi\rVert}}\rVert+\smash{\frac{2}{5}}\varepsilon

\mathllap{\smash{(\begin{subarray}{l}\textrm{Prop~\ref{prop:A1(1)}}\end{subarray})}}

\displaystyle\leq\int\lVert(k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}y)-k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x))\mathbbm{1}_{K}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}dh+\smash{\frac{2}{5}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\int\lVert(k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}y)-k(h,(\cdot)^{\smash{-1}}x))\mathbbm{1}_{K}(\cdot)\rVert_{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{F}(h)dh+\smash{\frac{4}{5}}\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\varepsilon\mathchoice{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{.\kern-2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\smash{\rule{1.26663pt}{1.26663pt}}\hss}\kern 2.11108pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss.}}{\text{.}}{\text{.}}\qed

Remark 4.1.1
.

Theorem
4.1
will be improved provided the group is amenable.

Let
\chi
be a compactly-supported continuous nonzero vector in
